def fibfib(n: int):
    """The FibFib number sequence is a sequence similar to the Fibbonacci sequnece that's defined as follows:
    fibfib(0) == 0
    fibfib(1) == 0
    fibfib(2) == 1
    fibfib(n) == fibfib(n-1) + fibfib(n-2) + fibfib(n-3).
    Please write a function to efficiently compute the n-th element of the fibfib number sequence.
    >>> fibfib(1)
    0
    >>> fibfib(5)
    4
    >>> fibfib(8)
    24
    """

def pluck(arr):
    """
    "Given an array representing a branch of a tree that has non-negative integer nodes
    your task is to pluck one of the nodes and return it.
    The plucked node should be the node with the smallest even value.
    If multiple nodes with the same smallest even value are found return the node that has smallest index.

    The plucked node should be returned in a list, [ smalest_value, its index ],
    If there are no even values or the given array is empty, return [].

    Example 1:
        Input: [4,2,3]
        Output: [2, 1]
        Explanation: 2 has the smallest even value, and 2 has the smallest index.

    Example 2:
        Input: [1,2,3]
        Output: [2, 1]
        Explanation: 2 has the smallest even value, and 2 has the smallest index. 

    Example 3:
        Input: []
        Output: []
    
    Example 4:
        Input: [5, 0, 3, 0, 4, 2]
        Output: [0, 1]
        Explanation: 0 is the smallest value, but  there are two zeros,
                     so we will choose the first zero, which has the smallest index.

    Constraints:
        * 1 <= nodes.length <= 10000
        * 0 <= node.value
    """

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list of odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture involves a sequence starting with n. Each term is derived from the 
    previous one: if it's even, the next term is half; if odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    plus 1. The conjecture suggests the sequence always reaches 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. The returned list is sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1 and 5.
    """

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
```python
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list of odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture involves a sequence: start with any positive integer n. If the term is even, 
    the next term is half of it. If odd, the next term is 3 times the term plus 1. The conjecture states 
    the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
```

        You are allowed to perform right shift operation any number of times.

    position in the right direction. The last element of the array will be moved to
    the starting position in the array i.e. 0th index. 

    If it is possible to obtain the sorted array by performing the above operation
    then return True else return False.
    If the given array is empty then return True.

    Note: The given list is guaranteed to have unique elements.

    For Example:
    
    move_one_ball([3, 4, 5, 1, 2])==>True

def move_one_ball(arr):
    """An array 'arr' of N integers arr[1], arr[2], ..., arr[N] is given.
    The numbers in the array are randomly ordered. Your objective is to find out if
    the array can be arranged in a non-decreasing order by repeatedly performing
    the following operation:
        You may execute a right shift operation as many times as needed.

    A right shift operation involves moving all elements of the array one
    position to the right. The last element of the array then shifts to
    the beginning of the array, at the 0th index.

    If you can obtain a sorted array by using the described operation,
    return True; otherwise, return False.
    If the array provided is empty, return True.

    Note: The list will consist of unique elements.

    For Example:

    move_one_ball([3, 4, 5, 1, 2])==>True
    Explanation: By executing 2 right shift operations, the array can be sorted
                 in non-decreasing order.
    move_one_ball([3, 5, 4, 1, 2])==>False
    Explanation: Achieving non-decreasing order for this array is impossible
                using any number of right shift operations.
                
    Consider edge cases where the list might already be sorted or contain a single element.
    Also, think about how you can exploit the properties of circular permutations to check for sorting eligibility efficiently.
    """

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    The implementation should ensure that repeated odd numbers are not included multiple times in the list.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

    return True; otherwise, return False.

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list of odd numbers in the Collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a mathematical sequence: start with any positive integer n. 
    If the term is even, the next term is half of it. If odd, the next term is 3 times it plus 1.
    The conjecture is that the sequence always reaches 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. Return the list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The Collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are 1 and 5.
    """

 def move_one_ball(arr):
    """You are provided with an array 'arr' of N integers: arr[1], arr[2], ..., arr[N]. 
    These numbers are arranged in random order. Your goal is to determine if
    it's possible to reorganize the array in a non-decreasing order by performing
    the following action multiple times:
        A right shift operation can be applied as often as necessary.
    
    In a right shift operation, each element of the array moves one
    position towards the right. The final element is then moved to
    the start of the array, taking the index position 0.

    If the described operation allows you to convert the array into a sorted one,
    return True; if not, return False.
    For an empty array, return True.

    Note: The array will have unique values.

    For Example:
    
    move_one_ball([3, 4, 5, 1, 2])==>True
    Explanation: By applying 2 right shift operations, the array becomes
                 non-decreasingly sorted.
    move_one_ball([3, 5, 4, 1, 2])==>False
    Explanation: It's not possible to sort this array in a non-decreasing order
                 using any amount of right shift operations.
                
    Consider edge cases where the array might be already sorted or have just one element.
    """

def move_one_ball(arr):
    """You are given an array 'arr' consisting of N integers: arr[1], arr[2], ..., arr[N]. 
    These integers are in a random sequence. Your task is to check if
    it's achievable to arrange the array in a non-decreasing order by applying
    the following process multiple times:
        You may perform a right shift operation as many times as needed.
    
    During a right shift operation, each element of the array is shifted
    one position to the right. The last element then moves to
    the beginning of the array, occupying the index position 0.

    If the mentioned operation helps you sort the array, return True; otherwise, return False.
    For an empty array, return True.

    Note: The values in the array are unique.

    For Example:
    
    move_one_ball([3, 4, 5, 1, 2])==>True
    Explanation: By carrying out 2 right shift operations, the array becomes
                 sorted in non-decreasing order.
    move_one_ball([3, 5, 4, 1, 2])==>False
    Explanation: It's impossible to sort this array in non-decreasing order
                 using any number of right shift operations.

def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """

mutated prompt:
def get_odd_collatz(n):
    """
    Given a positive integer n, return a sorted list that has the odd numbers in collatz sequence.

    The Collatz conjecture is a conjecture in mathematics that concerns a sequence defined
    as follows: start with any positive integer n. Then each term is obtained from the 
    previous term as follows: if the previous term is even, the next term is one half of 
    the previous term. If the previous term is odd, the next term is 3 times the previous
    term plus 1. The conjecture is that no matter what value of n, the sequence will always reach 1.

    Note: 
        1. Collatz(1) is [1].
        2. returned list sorted in increasing order.
        
    You can assume that the function input will always satisfy the given constraints.

    For example:
    get_odd_collatz(5) returns [1, 5] # The collatz sequence for 5 is [5, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1], so the odd numbers are only 1, and 5.
    """
